2. RECOGNITION 
       B.  2018 National Merit Scholarship Semifinalists  
This year, more than one million students , nationwide, from some 22,000 high 
schools, entered the National Merit Scholarship Program; however, only 16,000  of 
these seniors are selected to be semifinalists.  This evening , we will be passing a 
resolution to c ommend and congratulate our fifty-one National Merit Scholarship 
Semifinalists:   Sanjana Anil (Sun-juhn-ah A-nil), Brinda Avutapalli, Anuja Bagri (Uh-
noo-jah Baa-gree), Venkatesh Bajji (Ven-kha-teysh Bha-jee), Joseph Barlow,   
Tejas Bolar (Tay-jes Bo-lar), Michelle Cai (Michelle C-eye), Tuhin Chakraborty (Too-
hin Cha-kra-boar-tee), Annie Chen, Afriti Chinoy (Ahfr-ĕťee Chin-oi), Liam Clancy,  
Emmanuel Dannug (Emmanuel Dan-ugg), Manasi Desai (Mah-nuh-see Dhey-sigh), 
Allen Diao (Allen Dee -ow), Sahil Dogra (Sah-hill Doh-gruh), Raul Dutta (Ra-ul Du-
ta), James Ge (James Gee) , Kathleen Gu (Kat hleen Goo) , Lyndsey Gu (Lyndsey 
Goo), Claire Hao (Claire How), Runxuan Jiang (Run-shawn Jong), Yunsoo Kim (Yūn-
sōō Kĭm), Austin Lan, James Li (James Lee), Michael Lin, Spencer Liu (Spencer Loo), 
Kiran Menon (Key-run Men-nun), Anna Mitrofan (Anna Mih-troh-fan), Murali Mohan, 
Robert Mu , Akash Nadella (Uh -kaash Nah -dell-ah), Mohnish Nadella (Moh -neesh 
Nah-dell-ah), Andrew Nguyen (An-droo Noo-en), Shankar Prabhu (Shun-ker Prah-
bu), Ashka Pujara (Aash-kaa Pujara), Adithya Sanjay (Uh-di-thee-ya Sanjay),  
Yusuf Uzhunnan (Yoo-suf Uz-u-naan), Muyang Wang (Moo-young Wung),  
Angela Wang, Amanda Wewer, Jonathan Wong, Megan Worrel (Megan Werl), 
Elise Xia (Elise Shaw), Ashley Xu (Ashley Shoe), Zeyu Yang (Bill Yang), 
Himaneesh Yerrakalva (hi-MAHN YE-ruh-KAHL-vuh), Justin Zhang, Daniel Zheng, 
William Zhou (William Joe), Jane Zhou (Jane Jo), Lisa Zou (Lisa Zow); and this is a 
remarkable number of semifinalists , and a tribute to the staff and parents of the 
Troy School District.  Copies of this resolution will be presented to the students at a 
reception on November 15th. 
RECOMMENDATION:  Adopt the following resolution.

RESOLUTION 
2018  
National Merit Scholarship Semifinalists 
Whereas, the above-mentioned fifty-one students earned the prestigious honor of being 
among those selected, nationwide, as this year's National Merit Scholarship Semifinalists: 
Whereas, student achievement is honored through the National Merit Scholarship 
semifinalist and finalist recognition programs; and 
Whereas, National Merit students reach semifinalist status by scoring in the top one 
and one-half percent on the National Preliminary Scholastic Aptitude Test (PSAT) and 
earning comparable scores on the Scholastic Aptitude Test (SAT). 
Be It Therefore Resolved that the Tr oy School District Board of Education adopts 
this resolution in honor of the aforementioned Troy School District stud ents for their 
selection as 2018 National Merit Scholarship Semifinalists. 
Signed this seventeenth day of October, 2017.

Association of School Business 
Certificate of Excellence in Financial Reporting Program 
honored the Troy School  District for excellence in the 
preparation and issuance of a high quality Comprehensive 
Annual Financial Report (CAFR) for the fiscal year ended June 
30, 2016.  The Certificate of Excellence in Financial Reporting 
has been created by ASBO International to  enable business 
officials to achieve a high standard of financial reporting. We 
are very pleased to recognize Rick West, Assistant 
Superintendent for Business Services, and his hard -working 
staff for all their effort throughout the year and for their 
continued dedication to excellence. 
Michigan Association of School 
Boards’ Awards: Honor Board & 
Standard of Excellence Award, 
President Nancy Philippart –  Master 
Troy School District Board of Education with several honors that 
were received this past year as a result of  their commitment to 
excellence in governance. This evening, we are recognizing the Troy 
Board of Education for its Honor Board achievement and Standard 
of Excellence Award, both of which were presented by the Michigan 
Association of School Boards last June. We are also honoring Board 
President Nancy Philippart for achieving the MASB Master Platinum 
Award, which requires a minimum of 813 education credits. All of 
these honors place the Troy Board of Education as being among a 
handful of other Michigan schoo l district governing bodies that 
have demonstrated a true commitment to student achievement 
and exemplary leadership. The Troy community is fortunate to have 
seven Board members who are so committed to the concept of life-
long learning and positive policy making.

Jordan Harris , Assistant Superintendent, Employee Services, 
Staffing Update 
provided a brief update on teacher staffing for the 201 7/2018 
school year.  He mentioned the factors that influenced the number 
of teachers hired, which included retirements/resignations, full-
year leaves of absence, FTE status, and student enrollment.  
Mr. Schmidt asked if all positions have been filled.  Mr. Harris 
indicated that the hiring process is complete and that only a 
counselor position at IA -East is still open but they are working to 
get it filled soon.

:       7 
professional learning opportunities offered to teachers and 
administrators over the summer.   Mr. Dziatczak shared the four 
banners of the district’s vision to become world class by 2020 and 
the characteristics that define world class: Prioritize Learning, 
Empower Students, Attend to Well -Being, and Invest in Early 
Childhood.  He provided an example of the agenda listing all the 
learning opport unities and projects, which included technology 
integration, new pedagogies for deep learning, secondary math 
flexible learning spaces, TSD Literacy Project, and Camp oneTROY.  
He also shared about the welcome back event on the first day of 
professional de velopment where district staff heard keynote 
speaker George Couros then spent the remainder of the day 
participating in myriad PD sessions of their choice.   
provided an update on the District’s enrollment status and thanked 
the Central Enrollment team for all their hard work in enrolling the 
many new families who have joined the District.  He report ed on 
the District’s enrollment numbers as of September 19, 2017, which 
continue to grow.  Compared to last year, the elementary 
enrollment is up by 88 students, 6 additional middle school 
students, and 105 more high school students.  Overall, the District 
has 199 more students than last year.   
BUSINESS 
Weight Room Equipment Bid 
Athens and Troy High Schools be awarded to All-Pro Exercise in the 
amount of $80,138.00 and that this amount be approved as an 
expenditure of the of the 2016 Capital Projects Fund. 
Mr. West shared that the recommendation for this equipment was 
a collaborative effort with Mr. Jolly, Director Athletics and that the 
existing cardio equipment has reached end-of-life.   Mrs. Hammond 
inquired how the equipment would be used as part of the physical 
education (PE) curriculum.  Mr. Dziatczak confirmed  that it would 
be used in PE curriculum by high school students who take full 
credit of PE and intro to PE.

Reports from Student 
Representatives to the Board  
Nadia Shaouni, student representative from Troy College & Career 
High School  reported that 37 new students have come to Troy 
College & Career High School this mont h from  Troy and many 
surrounding metro -Detroit districts .  Student Government is 
holding general elections for leadership board and will be planning 
a Homecoming dance for next month.   
Anjana Pisupati,  student representative from International 
Academy –  East, reported that IA students have been in school for 
6 weeks already.  Seniors took a trip to Camp Tamarack for 
chemistry, biology  and physics and were combining their 
experiences doing experiments. IA students ar e holding a C -Day 
which is “ Give Back to the Community ”  and will be involved in 
gardening and working on things for the Pontiac and Detroit School 
Districts.  They ’ ve received a lot of positive feedback fr om doing 
that.  
Hana Barre, student representative from  Troy High School , 
reported that their Homecoming theme this year is Pixar.  The Intro 
to Leadership is a mentoring program for new students and senior 
interviews are being held by counselors.  Clubs at Troy High are just 
starting along with extracurricular programs.   
Tori Zarbaugh, student representative from  Athens High School , 
reported that Athens classes have been meeting with principals to 
promote getting involved and highlighted the four pillars on what it 
means to be World Class.  Homecoming is this weekend and the 
community is invited to attend.  
Dr. Philippart thanked the students for doing a great job this 
evening.

A. CONSIDERATION:  Sex Education Advisory Board Committee  
        Membership  
Districts must establish a Sex Education Advisory Board.  At least one -half of the 
board must be parents and one of the parents must co -chair the board.  A 
complete membership list for the 201 7/2018 school year  is attached for your 
consideration and it would be appropriate at this time for the Board of Education 
to adopt the following resolution.  
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below.
